<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes

   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes
   * If you create temporary files for testing, delete them after confirming your solution works

<SECURITY>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.

  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)

  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed

</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>

  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability

<DOCUMENTATION>

  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested

  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation

</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

1. This is about a bug in PyMarkdown related to rule Md031 (which seems to be about fenced code blocks)
2. The issue is with indents not aligning properly in a specific scenario involving:
   - Block quotes (`>`)
   - Nested lists (`+ list 1`, `+ list 2`)  
   - Paragraph continuation (`list 3`)
   - Thematic breaks (`------`)
   - Fenced code blocks (```block ... ```)
3. The actual vs expected behavior shows that the thematic breaks and code blocks are losing their proper indentation level

Looking at the markdown example:
```
> + list 1
>   + list 2
>   list 3
>   ------
>   ```block
>   A code block
>   ```
>   ------
> + another list
```

Expected formatting preserves the indentation:
- The thematic breaks (`------`) should remain indented at the same level as the list items
- The fenced code block should also maintain its indentation level

Actual formatting shows they lose indentation:
- Thematic breaks become flush left (`------` instead of `>   ------`)
- Code blocks lose their indentation too

This suggests that rule Md031 is incorrectly handling indentation when processing fenced code blocks within nested structures like blockquotes with lists.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue is with **rule Md031** in PyMarkdown that handles fenced code blocks. The problem manifests when:

- **Input structure**: A blockquote (`>`) containing nested lists with paragraph continuation and thematic breaks, followed by a fenced code block
- **Bug**: Indentation is not preserved properly for thematic breaks and fenced code blocks within complex nested structures
- **Technical details**: 
  - Rule name: `Md031` 
  - Context: fenced code blocks in lists within blockquotes
  - Error pattern: Loss of proper indentation levels

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce

1. Create a markdown document with the specific structure:
   ```
   > + list 1
   >   + list 2
   >   list 3
   >   ------
   >   ```block
   >   A code block
   >   ```
   >   ------
   > + another list
   ```
2. Process it with PyMarkdown (likely with rule Md031 enabled)
3. Observe that thematic breaks and code blocks lose their indentation

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing

- Understand the existing test structure for Md031 rules
- Create minimal reproduction cases
- Preserve existing functionality while fixing the indentation issue
- Test edge cases with various nesting levels

    10	
    11	## What Is PyMarkdown?
    12	
    13	PyMarkdown is primarily a Markdown linter. To ensure that the Markdown
    14	[linting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lint_%28software%29) is conducted with
    15	the highest attention to detail, our linting rules analyze the tokens produced
    16	by the project's own Markdown parser. The project's Markdown parser aims to be
    17	highly compliant to both the
    18	[GitHub Flavored Markdown](https://github.github.com/gfm/) specification and the
    19	[CommonMark](https://spec.commonmark.org/) specification. This is important as
    20	we estimate that over 90% of the existing parsers are mostly compliant to one of
    21	those two specifications. By our parser adhering to those specifications, we
    22	have a high degree of confidence that the rules we have written are based on
    23	solid information about the structure of the Markdown documents that they are
    24	evaluating. Since the rules have precise information on how compliant parsers
    25	view the structure of a given Markdown document, each rule can make the best
    26	decision possible on whether it should trigger a failure or not.
    27	
    28	## Why Should I Use PyMarkdown?
    29	
    30	We go into this in more detail in our [project documentation](https://pymarkdown.readthedocs.io/en/latest/),
    31	but here is the short version.
    32	
    33	Do you use spell checkers and grammer checkers for your emails, instant messages,
    34	and documents?
    35	
    36	Do you use static code analysis tools to verify that you are committing your best
    37	source code to your projects?
    38

   112	    def __fix_spacing_special_case(
   113	        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
   114	    ) -> None:
   115	        assert (
   116	            self.__last_token is not None
   117	        ), "Special case means at least a block token."
   118	        new_token = copy.deepcopy(token)
   119	        self.__fix_count += 1
   120	        new_token.adjust_line_number(context, self.__fix_count)
   121	        replacement_tokens = [
   122	            BlankLineMarkdownToken(
   123	                extracted_whitespace="", position_marker=PositionMarker(0, 0, "")
   124	            ),
   125	            self.__last_token,
   126	            new_token,
   127	        ]
   128	        self.register_replace_tokens_request(
   129	            context, self.__last_token, token, replacement_tokens
   130	        )
   131	        end_token = cast(EndMarkdownToken, self.__last_token)
   132	        block_quote_start_token = cast(
   133	            BlockQuoteMarkdownToken, end_token.start_markdown_token
   134	        )
   135	        assert (
   136	            block_quote_start_token.bleading_spaces is not None
   137	        ), "At least one line should have been processed."
   138	        split_bleading_spaces = block_quote_start_token.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
   139	        self.__container_adjustments[-1].append(
   140	            PendingContainerAdjustment(
   141	                len(split_bleading_spaces) - 1, split_bleading_spaces[-1].rstrip()
   142	            )
   143	        )
   144	
   145	    def __fix_spacing_block_quote(self, token: MarkdownToken) -> None:
   146	        container_index = len(self.__container_token_stack) - 1
   147	        block_quote_token = cast(
   148	            BlockQuoteMarkdownToken, self.__container_token_stack[container_index]
   149	        )
   150	        assert (
   151	            block_quote_token.bleading_spaces is not None
   152	        ), "At least one line should have been processed."
   153	        split_leading_space = block_quote_token.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
   154	        if token.is_setext_heading:
   155	            setext_token = cast(SetextHeadingMarkdownToken, token)
   156	            token_line_number = setext_token.original_line_number
   157	        else:
   158	            token_line_number = token.line_number
   159	        leading_space_insert_index = (
   160	            token_line_number - block_quote_token.line_number
   161	        ) - self.__closed_container_adjustments[-1].adjustment
   162	        former_item_leading_space = split_leading_space[
   163	            leading_space_insert_index
   164	        ].rstrip()
   165	        self.__container_adjustments[container_index].append(
   166	            PendingContainerAdjustment(
   167	                leading_space_insert_index, former_item_leading_space
   168	            )
   169	        )
   170	
   171	        while (
   172	            container_index > 0
   173	            and not self.__container_token_stack[container_index - 1].is_list_start
   174	        ):
   175	            container_index -= 1
   176	
   177	        if (
   178	            container_index > 0
   179	            and self.__container_token_stack[container_index - 1].is_list_start
   180	        ):
   181	            if token.is_setext_heading:
   182	                setext_token = cast(SetextHeadingMarkdownToken, token)
   183	                token_line_number = setext_token.original_line_number
   184	            else:
   185	                token_line_number = token.line_number
   186	            leading_space_insert_index = (
   187	                token_line_number
   188	                - self.__container_token_stack[container_index - 1].line_number
   189	            )
   190	            self.__container_adjustments[container_index - 1].append(
   191	                PendingContainerAdjustment(leading_space_insert_index, "")
   192	            )
   193	
   194	    def __fix_spacing_list(self, token: MarkdownToken) -> None:
   195	        initial_index = container_index = len(self.__container_token_stack) - 1
   196	        while (
   197	            container_index > 0
   198	            and self.__container_token_stack[container_index - 1].is_list_start
   199	        ):
   200	            container_index -= 1
   201	        if container_index:

   204	                token, container_index, initial_index
   205	            )
   206	
   207	            assert block_quote_index.bleading_spaces is not None
   208	            split_bleading_spaces = block_quote_index.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
   209	            self.__container_adjustments[container_index - 1].append(
   210	                PendingContainerAdjustment(index, split_bleading_spaces[index].rstrip())
   211	            )
   212	            # this may be due to a commented out test

   218	        adjust = self.__calculate_adjust(initial_index, container_index)

   221	            token_line_number = setext_token.original_line_number

   224	        index = (

   227	        index -= self.__closed_container_adjustments[-1].adjustment
   228	        self.__container_adjustments[initial_index].append(
   229	            PendingContainerAdjustment(index - adjust, "")
   230	        )

   232	    def __xxxx(
   233	        self,
   234	        token: MarkdownToken,
   235	        container_index: int,
   236	        initial_index: int,

   238	        block_quote_index = cast(

   241	        )
   242	        if token.is_setext_heading:

   252	        df = self.__closed_container_adjustments[container_index - 1].adjustment
   253	        ff = df != 0

   256

   275	    def __calculate_adjust(self, initial_index: int, container_index: int) -> int:
   276	        if (
   277	            initial_index >= 2
   278	            and not container_index
   279	            and self.__closed_container_adjustments[-1].adjustment
   280	        ):
   281	            return 1
   282	        return (
   283	            0
   284	            if initial_index >= 1
   285	            and not container_index
   286	            and self.__closed_container_adjustments[-1].adjustment
   287	            else 1
   288	        )

   290	    def __fix_spacing(
   291	        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken, special_case: bool
   292	    ) -> None:
   293	        if special_case:
   294	            self.__fix_spacing_special_case(context, token)
   295	            return
   296	        if self.__container_token_stack:
   297	            if self.__container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start:
   298	                self.__fix_spacing_block_quote(token)
   299	            else:
   300	                self.__fix_spacing_list(token)
   301	
   302	        new_token = copy.deepcopy(token)
   303	        self.__fix_count += 1
   304	        new_token.adjust_line_number(context, self.__fix_count)
   305	        replacement_tokens = [
   306	            BlankLineMarkdownToken(
   307	                extracted_whitespace="",
   308	                position_marker=PositionMarker(new_token.line_number - 1, 0, ""),
   309	                column_delta=1,
   310	            ),
   311	            new_token,
   312	        ]
   313	        self.register_replace_tokens_request(context, token, token, replacement_tokens)
   314	
   315	    def __handle_fenced_code_block(
   316	        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken, special_case: bool
   317	    ) -> None:
   318	
   319	        can_trigger = (
   320	            self.__trigger_in_list_items
   321	            if self.__container_token_stack
   322	            and self.__container_token_stack[-1].is_list_start
   323	            else True
   324	        )
   325	        if (
   326	            self.__last_non_end_token
   327	            and not self.__last_non_end_token.is_blank_line
   328	            and can_trigger
   329	        ):
   330	            if context.in_fix_mode:
   331	                self.__fix_spacing(context, token, special_case)
   332	            else:
   333	                self.report_next_token_error(context, token)
   334	
   335	    def __calculate_end_deltas(self) -> Tuple[int, int]:
   336	        line_number_delta = 0
   337	        assert self.__last_non_end_token is not None
   338	        if self.__last_non_end_token.is_text:
   339	            text_token = cast(TextMarkdownToken, self.__last_non_end_token)
   340	            line_number_delta = (
   341	                text_token.token_text.count(ParserHelper.newline_character) + 2
   342	            )
   343	        else:
   344	            assert self.__last_non_end_token.is_fenced_code_block
   345	            line_number_delta = 1
   346	
   347	        assert self.__end_fenced_code_block_token is not None
   348	        column_number_delta = (
   349	            self.__end_fenced_code_block_token.start_markdown_token.column_number
   350	        )
   351	        start_token = cast(
   352	            EndMarkdownToken,
   353	            self.__end_fenced_code_block_token.start_markdown_token,
   354	        )
   355	        if start_token.extracted_whitespace:
   356	            column_number_delta -= len(start_token.extracted_whitespace)
   357	        if (
   358	            self.__end_fenced_code_block_token
   359	            and self.__end_fenced_code_block_token.extracted_whitespace
   360	        ):
   361	            column_number_delta += len(
   362	                self.__end_fenced_code_block_token.extracted_whitespace
   363	            )
   364	        column_number_delta = -(column_number_delta)
   365	
   366	        return line_number_delta, column_number_delta
   367	
   368	    def __handle_end_fenced_code_block(
   369	        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
   370	    ) -> None:  # sourcery skip: extract-method
   371	        can_trigger = not token.is_end_of_stream
   372	        if (
   373	            self.__container_token_stack
   374	            and self.__container_token_stack[-1].is_list_start
   375	        ):
   376	            can_trigger = self.__trigger_in_list_items
   377	        if (
   378	            not token.is_blank_line
   379	            and self.__end_fenced_code_block_token is not None
   380	            and not self.__end_fenced_code_block_token.was_forced
   381	            and can_trigger
   382	        ):

   438	    def __process_pending_container_end_block_quote(self, token: MarkdownToken) -> None:
   439	        for stack_index in range(len(self.__container_token_stack) - 2, -1, -1):
   440	            current_stack_token = self.__container_token_stack[stack_index]
   441	            if current_stack_token.is_block_quote_start:
   442	                if token.is_setext_heading:
   443	                    setext_token = cast(SetextHeadingMarkdownToken, token)
   444	                    token_line_number = setext_token.original_line_number
   445	                else:
   446	                    token_line_number = token.line_number
   447	                line_number_delta = (
   448	                    token_line_number - self.__container_token_stack[-1].line_number
   449	                )
   450	                extra_end_data = self.__end_tokens[-1].extra_end_data

   453	                self.__closed_container_adjustments[
   454	                    stack_index
   455	                ].adjustment += line_number_delta
   456	                self.__closed_container_adjustments[stack_index].count += 1
   457	                break
   458	
   459	    def __process_pending_container_end_list(self, token: MarkdownToken) -> None:
   460	        for stack_index in range(len(self.__container_token_stack) - 2, -1, -1):
   461	            current_stack_token = self.__container_token_stack[stack_index]
   462	            if current_stack_token.is_list_start:
   463	                if token.is_setext_heading:
   464	                    setext_token = cast(SetextHeadingMarkdownToken, token)
   465	                    token_line_number = setext_token.original_line_number
   466	                else:
   467	                    token_line_number = token.line_number
   468	                line_number_delta = (
   469	                    token_line_number - self.__container_token_stack[-1].line_number
   470	                )
   471	                self.__closed_container_adjustments[
   472	                    stack_index
   473	                ].adjustment += line_number_delta
   474	                break
   475	
   476	    def __process_pending_container_end(
   477	        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
   478	    ) -> None:
   479	        if next_container_adjustment_list := self.__container_adjustments[-1]:
   480	            self.__process_pending_container_end_adjustment(
   481	                context, next_container_adjustment_list
   482	            )
   483	
   484	        if self.__container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start:
   485	            self.__process_pending_container_end_block_quote(token)
   486	        else:
   487	            self.__process_pending_container_end_list(token)
   488	
   489	        del self.__container_token_stack[-1]
   490	        del self.__container_adjustments[-1]
   491	        del self.__closed_container_adjustments[-1]
   492	        del self.__end_tokens[-1]
   493	        self.__pending_container_ends -= 1
   494	
   495	    def __calculate_special_case(
   496	        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
   497	    ) -> bool:
   498	        return bool(
   499	            context.in_fix_mode
   500	            and token.is_fenced_code_block
   501	            and len(self.__container_token_stack) >= 2
   502	            and self.__container_token_stack[-1].is_block_quote_start
   503	            and self.__container_token_stack[-2].is_block_quote_start
   504	            and self.__last_token
   505	            and self.__second_last_token
   506	            and self.__last_token.is_block_quote_end
   507	            and self.__second_last_token.is_paragraph_end
   508	        )
   509	
   510	    def next_token(self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken) -> None:
   511	        """
   512	        Event that a new token is being processed.
   513	        """
   514	
   515	        special_case = self.__calculate_special_case(context, token)
   516	        # special_case = False
   517	
   518	        if not token.is_end_token or token.is_end_of_stream:
   519	            while self.__pending_container_ends and not special_case:
   520	                self.__process_pending_container_end(context, token)
   521	            if self.__end_fenced_code_block_token:
   522	                self.__handle_end_fenced_code_block(context, token)
   523	
   524	        if token.is_block_quote_start or token.is_list_start:
   525	            self.__container_token_stack.append(token)
   526	            self.__container_adjustments.append([])
   527	            self.__closed_container_adjustments.append(ClosedContainerAdjustments())
   528	        elif token.is_block_quote_end or token.is_list_end:
   529	            self.__pending_container_ends += 1
   530	            self.__end_tokens.append(cast(EndMarkdownToken, token))
   531	        elif token.is_fenced_code_block:
   532	            self.__handle_fenced_code_block(context, token, special_case)
   533	            while self.__pending_container_ends and special_case:
   534	                self.__process_pending_container_end(context, token)
   535	        elif token.is_fenced_code_block_end:
   536	            self.__end_fenced_code_block_token = cast(EndMarkdownToken, token)
   537	
   538	        if (
   539	            not token.is_end_token
   540	            and not token.is_block_quote_start
   541	            and not token.is_list_start
   542	        ):
   543	            self.__last_non_end_token = token
   544	
   545	        self.__second_last_token = self.__last_token
   546	        self.__last_token = token
   547

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/test_reproduction.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""
Script to reproduce the indentation issue with Md031 rule.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess

def test_md031_indent_issue():
    """Test the specific indentation issue described in the bug report."""
    
    # Input markdown that triggers the issue
    input_markdown = """> + list 1
>   + list 2
>   list 3
>   ------
>   ```block
>   A code block
>   ```
>   ------
> + another list
"""

    # Expected output according to the bug report
    expected_output = """> + list 1
>   + list 2
>   list 3
>   ------
>
>   ```block
>   A code block
>   ```
>
>   ------
> + another list
"""

    print("=== INPUT MARKDOWN ===")
    print(input_markdown)
    print("=====================")
    
    # Create a temporary file with the input
    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', suffix='.md', delete=False) as f:
        f.write(input_markdown)
        temp_file = f.name
    
    try:
        # Run pymarkdown fix command
        result = subprocess.run([
            'python', '-m', 'pymarkdown', '--log-level', 'DEBUG', 'fix', temp_file
        ], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd='/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9')
        
        print("=== COMMAND OUTPUT ===")
        print("STDOUT:", result.stdout)
        print("STDERR:", result.stderr)
        print("Return code:", result.returncode)
        print("======================")
        
        # Read the fixed file
        with open(temp_file, 'r') as f:
            fixed_content = f.read()
            
        print("=== ACTUAL FIXED CONTENT ===")
        print(repr(fixed_content))
        print("=== ACTUAL FIXED CONTENT (formatted) ===")
        print(fixed_content)
        print("==============================")
        
        print("=== EXPECTED CONTENT ===")
        print(repr(expected_output))
        print("=== EXPECTED CONTENT (formatted) ===")
        print(expected_output)
        print("=========================")
        
        # Check if the issue is reproduced
        if fixed_content == expected_output:
            print("✅ ISSUE IS FIXED!")
            return True
        else:
            print("❌ ISSUE STILL EXISTS!")
            print("Differences:")
            actual_lines = fixed_content.split('\n')
            expected_lines = expected_output.split('\n')
            for i, (actual, expected) in enumerate(zip(actual_lines, expected_lines)):
                if actual != expected:
                    print(f"  Line {i+1}:")
                    print(f"    Actual  : {repr(actual)}")
                    print(f"    Expected: {repr(expected)}")
            return False
            
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(temp_file)

if __name__ == "__main__":

   - When the fix inserts blank lines, it's not preserving the proper leading spaces for the surrounding elements
   - The indentation calculation logic seems to be failing in this specific nested context

   - The fix should analyze the current container stack and apply the correct leading spaces

Looking at the debug output, I can see that the rule is correctly identifying the violation but the fix mechanism is not preserving the indentation properly.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The Md031 rule correctly identifies that fenced code blocks need blank lines around them, but when automatically fixing the issue, it fails to preserve the proper indentation of surrounding elements (thematic breaks and code block markers) within nested structures like blockquotes containing lists.

### 5.2 Problem Location
The issue is in `/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/pymarkdown/plugins/rule_md_031.py`, specifically in the `__fix_spacing` method and related container adjustment functions.

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
The reproduction script shows that when fixing:
```
> + list 1
>   + list 2
>   list 3
>   ------
>   ```block
>   A code block
>   ```
>   ------
> + another list
```

The thematic breaks (`------`) and code block markers (```block and ```) lose their indentation, becoming `> ------` instead of `>   ------`.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Preserve existing indentation patterns when inserting blank lines
- Respect the container hierarchy (blockquotes, lists) when calculating proper leading spaces
- Ensure that the fix doesn't break other existing functionality
- Maintain consistency with how other similar rules handle indentation

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem
Looking at the code, the issue is in the `__fix_spacing` method where new `BlankLineMarkdownToken` objects are created without proper consideration of the current container indentation. The fix needs to:

1. Calculate the proper leading whitespace based on the current container stack
2. Apply this leading whitespace to the newly inserted blank lines
3. Ensure that subsequent tokens maintain their proper indentation levels

    def __calculate_proper_indentation(self) -> str:
        """
        Calculate the proper leading whitespace/indentation for a blank line
        based on the current container stack.
        """
        if not self.__container_token_stack:
            return ""

        # Start with empty indentation
        leading_spaces = []
        
        # Process each container in the stack to build proper indentation
        for container_token in self.__container_token_stack:
            if container_token.is_block_quote_start:
                block_quote_token = cast(BlockQuoteMarkdownToken, container_token)
                # For block quotes, we need to get the appropriate leading space
                # In many cases, this would be "> " or ">   " depending on nesting
                if block_quote_token.bleading_spaces:
                    split_spaces = block_quote_token.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
                    # Use the last known leading space, or a default "> "
                    if split_spaces and split_spaces[-1]:
                        leading_spaces.append(split_spaces[-1])
                    else:
                        leading_spaces.append("> ")
                else:
                    leading_spaces.append("> ")
            elif container_token.is_list_start:
                list_token = cast(ListStartMarkdownToken, container_token)
                # For lists, we need to get the appropriate leading space
                if list_token.leading_spaces:
                    split_spaces = list_token.leading_spaces.split("\n")
                    # Use the last known leading space
                    if split_spaces and split_spaces[-1]:
                        leading_spaces.append(split_spaces[-1])
                    else:
                        # Default to some spaces based on list indentation
                        leading_spaces.append("  ")  # TODO: Calculate properly
                else:
                    leading_spaces.append("  ")

        # Combine all leading spaces
        if leading_spaces:
            # Join with appropriate separators, but be careful about duplicates
            combined = "".join(leading_spaces)
            return combined
        return ""

    def __calculate_special_case(
        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
    ) -> bool:</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/pymarkdown/plugins/rule_md_031.py has been edited. Here's the result of running `cat -n` on a snippet of /workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/pymarkdown/plugins/rule_md_031.py:

    def __calculate_proper_indentation(self) -> str:
        """
        Calculate the proper leading whitespace/indentation for a blank line
        based on the current container stack.
        """
        if not self.__container_token_stack:
            return ""

        # Start with empty indentation
        leading_spaces = []
        
        # Process each container in the stack to build proper indentation
        for container_token in self.__container_token_stack:
            if container_token.is_block_quote_start:
                block_quote_token = cast(BlockQuoteMarkdownToken, container_token)
                # For block quotes, we need to get the appropriate leading space
                # In many cases, this would be "> " or ">   " depending on nesting
                if block_quote_token.bleading_spaces:
                    split_spaces = block_quote_token.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
                    # Use the last known leading space, or a default "> "
                    if split_spaces and split_spaces[-1]:
                        leading_spaces.append(split_spaces[-1])
                    else:
                        leading_spaces.append("> ")
                else:
                    leading_spaces.append("> ")
            elif container_token.is_list_start:
                list_token = cast(ListStartMarkdownToken, container_token)
                # For lists, we need to get the appropriate leading space
                if list_token.leading_spaces:
                    split_spaces = list_token.leading_spaces.split("\n")
                    # Use the last known leading space
                    if split_spaces and split_spaces[-1]:
                        leading_spaces.append(split_spaces[-1])
                    else:
                        # Default to some spaces based on list indentation
                        leading_spaces.append("  ")  # TODO: Calculate properly
                else:
                    leading_spaces.append("  ")

        # Combine all leading spaces
        if leading_spaces:
            # Join with appropriate separators, but be careful about duplicates
            combined = "".join(leading_spaces)
            return combined
        return ""

Let me add it after the `__process_pending_container_end` method:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/jackdewinter__pymarkdown__0.9/pymarkdown/plugins/rule_md_031.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>        self.__pending_container_ends -= 1

    def __apply_pending_container_adjustments(
        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
    ) -> None:
        """
        Apply pending container adjustments without removing containers from the stack.
        This ensures that tokens inserted after blank lines get proper indentation.
        """
        # Apply adjustments for all containers in the stack
        for container_index in range(len(self.__container_adjustments)):
            if next_container_adjustment_list := self.__container_adjustments[container_index]:
                # Process just the adjustments, not the full container end logic
                self.__process_pending_container_end_adjustment_only(
                    context, next_container_adjustment_list, container_index
                )

    def __process_pending_container_end_adjustment_only(
        self,
        context: PluginScanContext,
        next_container_adjustment_list: List[PendingContainerAdjustment],
        container_index: int,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Process container adjustments without removing containers from the stack.
        """
        if self.__container_token_stack[container_index].is_block_quote_start:
            token_part_name = "bleading_spaces"
            block_quote_token = cast(
                BlockQuoteMarkdownToken, self.__container_token_stack[container_index]
            )
            assert (
                block_quote_token.bleading_spaces is not None
            ), "Pending containers means this should at least have a newline in it."
            split_spaces = block_quote_token.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
        else:
            token_part_name = "leading_spaces"
            list_token = cast(ListStartMarkdownToken, self.__container_token_stack[container_index])
            assert (
                list_token.leading_spaces is not None

    def __apply_pending_container_adjustments(
        self, context: PluginScanContext, token: MarkdownToken
    ) -> None:
        """
        Apply pending container adjustments without removing containers from the stack.
        This ensures that tokens inserted after blank lines get proper indentation.
        """
        # Apply adjustments for all containers in the stack
        for container_index in range(len(self.__container_adjustments)):
            if next_container_adjustment_list := self.__container_adjustments[container_index]:
                # Process just the adjustments, not the full container end logic
                self.__process_pending_container_end_adjustment_only(
                    context, next_container_adjustment_list, container_index
                )

    def __process_pending_container_end_adjustment_only(
        self,
        context: PluginScanContext,
        next_container_adjustment_list: List[PendingContainerAdjustment],
        container_index: int,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Process container adjustments without removing containers from the stack.
        """
        if self.__container_token_stack[container_index].is_block_quote_start:
            token_part_name = "bleading_spaces"
            block_quote_token = cast(
                BlockQuoteMarkdownToken, self.__container_token_stack[container_index]
            )
            assert (
                block_quote_token.bleading_spaces is not None
            ), "Pending containers means this should at least have a newline in it."
            split_spaces = block_quote_token.bleading_spaces.split("\n")
        else:
            token_part_name = "leading_spaces"
            list_token = cast(ListStartMarkdownToken, self.__container_token_stack[container_index])
            assert (
                list_token.leading_spaces is not None
            ), "Pending containers means this should at least have a newline in it."
            split_spaces = list_token.leading_spaces.split("\n")

        for next_container_adjustment in next_container_adjustment_list[::-1]:
            # this may be due to a commented out test
            assert next_container_adjustment.do_insert
            split_spaces.insert(
                next_container_adjustment.insert_index,
                next_container_adjustment.leading_space_to_insert,
            )

        self.register_fix_token_request(
            context,
            self.__container_token_stack[container_index],
            "next_token",
            token_part_name,
            "\n".join(split_spaces),
        )

    def __apply_new_container_adjustments(
        self, 
        context: PluginScanContext, 
        original_adjustments: List[List[PendingContainerAdjustment]]
    ) -> None:
        """
        Apply container adjustments that were calculated in __fix_spacing_list
        but not present in the original adjustments.
        """
        for i, (original, current) in enumerate(zip(original_adjustments, self.__container_adjustments)):
            # Find adjustments that were added
            new_adjustments = current[len(original):]
            if new_adjustments:
                # Create a temporary list with just the new adjustments
                temp_adjustments = new_adjustments[:]
                if temp_adjustments:
                    self.__process_pending_container_end_adjustment(context, temp_adjustments)

The solution is to make sure that when I process the adjustments early, I prevent the normal logic from processing them again.

One way to do this is to clear the adjustments after processing them, which is what I tried to do. But the issue is that I might be clearing them at the wrong time or in the wrong way.

Let me look at the error more carefully. It says that both attempts are trying to modify the SAME FIELD of the SAME TOKEN. So it's not that I'm applying adjustments to different containers - it's that I'm applying adjustments to the same container twice.

Looking at my code, in `__apply_new_container_adjustments`, I'm processing adjustments for multiple containers. But if the same container gets adjusted twice (once by my early processing and once by the normal ending logic), that would cause the conflict.

What I should do is make sure that when I process an adjustment early, I mark it as processed so the normal logic skips it.

But a simpler approach might be to just prevent the normal logic from running if I've already processed the adjustments.

Actually, let me look at this from a different angle. The conflict error shows two different values being set for `leading_spaces`:
1. `'  \n\n  \n  \n  \n'`
2. `'  \n  \n  \n  \n>\n'`

The second one looks more correct - it has the extra `>\n` at the end, which would correspond to the blank line I'm inserting.

This suggests that my early processing is computing the right adjustment, but the normal ending logic is computing a slightly different adjustment.

The solution is to make sure that when I process adjustments early, I prevent the normal logic from processing the same adjustments.

Let me modify my approach. Instead of trying to be clever about which adjustments are new, let me just process ALL pending adjustments immediately after calculating them, and then clear them to prevent double-processing.
